# Every lead, 2026-09-24

**239 leads.** Join dossiers: 8 · The full menu: 111 · Deep pass 3, graded B: 84 · Deep pass 3, graded C or D: 36

Each lead: a plain headline, the number, the tables, the columns that carry it,
the join key, the grade, the next check, and the catch.
Columns come from the saved SQL. Where none was saved, the line says "not recorded".
7 leads have at least one table with columns not recorded.

Every person or company named is a data match, not verified against primary records. Nothing here is published.
Some leads show up in two sections; the "Same story as" line links them.

## Join dossiers: 8 leads

Traced across several agencies' files, then attacked by a skeptic.

### 1. Las Vegas Sands reported $20.58M in Texas lobby "media" spending, 2021-26: 2.5 times all other lobbyists combined
- **The number:** $20,575,810.66 over 62 monthly reports by one lobbyist, Andy Abboud, May 2021-Jun 2026: 70-71% of all Texas lobby media (everyone else $8.26M), about 4x Sands' ~$5.1M federal lobby fees in the same months
- **Tables:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__TX_LOBBY_COVER`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__TX_LOBBY_INDIVIDUAL_REPORTING`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.EDUCATION.EDUCATION__FED_SENATE_LDA_FILINGS`
- **Columns:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__TX_LOBBY_COVER`: REPORT_INFO_IDENT, FILER_IDENT, FILER_NAME, TOTAL_EXPEND_MEDIA, PERIOD_START_DT, PERIOD_END_DT, FILED_DT, REPORT_TYPE_CD · `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__TX_LOBBY_INDIVIDUAL_REPORTING`: REPORT_ID, ONBEHALFNAME, FILER_ID · `LIBRARY_MARTS.EDUCATION.EDUCATION__FED_SENATE_LDA_FILINGS`: CLIENT_NAME, REGISTRANT_NAME, FILING_YEAR, FILING_PERIOD, INCOME, DT_POSTED, SPECIFIC_ISSUES
- **Joined on:** `REPORT_ID` = `REPORT_INFO_IDENT` (Texas client lines to cover reports); federal lobbying matched on client name only, no key join
- **Grade:** A · confirmed · dossier j6 + j6_skeptic
- **Next:** Pull the Texas Ethics Commission's filing rules for the "media" line: ad buys, production, or pass-through to an agency?
- **Watch out:** Abboud is a Sands employee (general knowledge, unchecked), so this may be Sands' own ad campaign filed under his name; don't write "a lobbyist spent."
- **Same story as:** menu #10 (Sands Texas lobby media)

### 2. Reliant Care's 30 Missouri nursing homes log 35% fewer nurse hours and 3.8x the harm citations per bed
- **The number:** 2.16 vs 3.35 nurse hours per resident per day against other Missouri for-profit homes; 4.67 vs 1.23 harm citations per 100 beds (3.8x); fines $117,358 vs $27,138 per 100 beds (4.3x)
- **Tables:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME_DEFICIENCIES`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME_PENALTIES`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_SKILLED_NURSING_FACILITY_ENROLLMENTS`, `LIBRARY_RAW.LANDING.FED_CMS_SNF_OWNERSHIP`
- **Columns:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME`: CMS_CERTIFICATION_NUMBER_CCN, CHAIN_ID, STATE, OWNERSHIP_TYPE, NUMBER_OF_CERTIFIED_BEDS, AVERAGE_NUMBER_OF_RESIDENTS_PER_DAY, REPORTED_TOTAL_NURSE_STAFFING_HOURS_PER_RESIDENT_PER_DAY, REPORTED_RN_STAFFING_HOURS_PER_RESIDENT_PER_DAY, ADJUSTED_TOTAL_NURSE_STAFFING_HOURS_PER_RESIDENT_PER_DAY, NURSING_CASE_MIX_INDEX · `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME_DEFICIENCIES`: CMS_CERTIFICATION_NUMBER_CCN, STATE, SURVEY_DATE, SCOPE_SEVERITY_CODE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_NURSING_HOME_PENALTIES`: CMS_CERTIFICATION_NUMBER_CCN, STATE, PENALTY_DATE, PENALTY_TYPE, FINE_ID, FINE_AMOUNT · `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_SKILLED_NURSING_FACILITY_ENROLLMENTS`: CCN, ENROLLMENT_ID · `LIBRARY_RAW.LANDING.FED_CMS_SNF_OWNERSHIP`: ENROLLMENT_ID, ASSOCIATE_ID_OWNER, FIRST_NAME_OWNER, LAST_NAME_OWNER, ROLE_TEXT_OWNER, ASSOCIATION_DATE_OWNER, PERCENTAGE_OWNERSHIP
- **Joined on:** `CCN` (`CMS_CERTIFICATION_NUMBER_CCN`, padded to 6) across roster, citations, fines and enrollments; enrollments to the owner file on `ENROLLMENT_ID`
- **Grade:** B · narrowed · dossier j1 + j1_skeptic
- **Next:** Read the harm citation texts on Care Compare for the 4 homes carrying half the harm: Bridgewood, North Village Park, Heritage, Gregory Ridge
- **Watch out:** Those 4 worst-list homes hold 76 of 141 harm citations; without them harm and fines are 2.5x, not 3.8x/4.3x. And it's 30 homes, not 31.
- **Same story as:** menu #32 (Reliant Care); deep3 #2 and #50 fold into it

### 3. Grain Processing's Iowa corn mill failed smokestack tests 49 days in 2015-25, no state order; Indiana twin: 7 orders
- **The number:** Muscatine IA 49 failed-test days (#1 of 29 big US wet corn mills), 36 violations, 0 orders; Washington IN 20 days, 7 Indiana orders, $687,030. Statewide 2015-25: Iowa 19 state air orders, Indiana 572
- **Tables:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FACILITIES`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_STACK_TESTS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_VIOLATION_HISTORY`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FORMAL_ACTIONS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_GHGRP_FACILITY`
- **Columns:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FACILITIES`: PGM_SYS_ID, REGISTRY_ID, FACILITY_NAME, STATE, NAICS_CODES, AIR_POLLUTANT_CLASS_CODE, AIR_OPERATING_STATUS_CODE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_STACK_TESTS`: PGM_SYS_ID, ACTUAL_END_DATE, AIR_STACK_TEST_STATUS_CODE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_VIOLATION_HISTORY`: PGM_SYS_ID, HPV_DAYZERO_DATE, EARLIEST_FRV_DETERM_DATE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FORMAL_ACTIONS`: PGM_SYS_ID, SETTLEMENT_ENTERED_DATE, STATE_EPA_FLAG, PENALTY_AMOUNT, ENF_TYPE_DESC · `LIBRARY_MARTS.ENVIRONMENT.ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_GHGRP_FACILITY`: FRS_ID, PARENT_COMPANY, REPORTING_YEAR
- **Joined on:** `PGM_SYS_ID` (EPA air program ID) across the air files; `FRS_ID` = `REGISTRY_ID` to the greenhouse-gas file, where both plants list parent "KENT CORP (100%)"
- **Grade:** B · narrowed · dossier j7 + j7_skeptic
- **Next:** Search Iowa DNR's own enforcement records for any order against the Muscatine plant since 2008 that never reached EPA's file
- **Watch out:** The gap mirrors the two states, not this plant: Iowa barely files air orders at all. Split the Indiana mills' own 22 orders ($898,842) from EPA's 3 ($1.8M).
- **Same story as:** menu #24 (Iowa plants fail stack tests, Muscatine named)

### 4. In 2024, 20 clinicians billed Medicare $1.13B for skin-substitute wound patches; the makers reported paying them only $76,937
- **The number:** $1,126,228,007 billed by the top 20 in 2024 vs $76,937 in maker payments 2022-24 ($63,088 of it Organogenesis to Ravi Kapadia); 6 Arizona nurse practitioners billed only Impax, $124.0M (68.6% of all Impax)
- **Tables:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_PHYSICIAN_OTHER_PRACTITIONERS_BY_PROVIDER_AND_SERVI`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_OPEN_PAYMENTS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_OPEN_PAYMENTS_2023`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_OPEN_PAYMENTS_2022`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.PROCUREMENT.PROCUREMENT__FED_SAM_EXCLUSIONS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.JUSTICE.JUSTICE__FED_FJC_IDB_CRIMINAL`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.JUSTICE.JUSTICE__FED_COURTLISTENER_DOCKETS`
- **Columns:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_PHYSICIAN_OTHER_PRACTITIONERS_BY_PROVIDER_AND_SERVI`: RNDRNG_NPI, HCPCS_CD, HCPCS_DESC, AVG_MDCR_ALOWD_AMT, TOT_SRVCS, TOT_BENES, RNDRNG_PRVDR_LAST_ORG_NAME, RNDRNG_PRVDR_FIRST_NAME, RNDRNG_PRVDR_CITY, RNDRNG_PRVDR_STATE_ABRVTN · `LIBRARY_MARTS.HEALTH.HEALTH__FED_CMS_OPEN_PAYMENTS` (same columns in `_2023`, `_2022`): NPI, PROGRAM_YEAR, APPLICABLE_MANUFACTURER_OR_APPLICABLE_GPO_MAKING_PAYMENT_NAME, NATURE_OF_PAYMENT_OR_TRANSFER_OF_VALUE, TOTAL_AMOUNT_OF_PAYMENT_USDOLLARS · `LIBRARY_MARTS.PROCUREMENT.PROCUREMENT__FED_SAM_EXCLUSIONS`: NPI, FIRST_NAME, LAST_NAME, STATE, CITY, EXCLUSION_TYPE, EXCLUDING_AGENCY, ACTIVATION_DATE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.JUSTICE.JUSTICE__FED_FJC_IDB_CRIMINAL`: DISTRICT, OFFICE, DOCKET, FILE_DATE, FILING_TITLE_1, CT_FIL, STATUS_CODE · `LIBRARY_MARTS.JUSTICE.JUSTICE__FED_COURTLISTENER_DOCKETS`: COURT_ID, DOCKET_NUMBER, DATE_FILED, CASE_NAME
- **Joined on:** `NPI` (Part B `RNDRNG_NPI` = Open Payments `NPI`); SAM on first + last name + state, city checked; court docket to FJC on `DISTRICT` + `OFFICE` + `DOCKET`
- **Grade:** B · narrowed · dossier j3 + j3_skeptic
- **Next:** Pull the charging papers for Arizona federal cases 2:25-cr-00944 and 2:25-cr-00947 to see if they name Denny and Palacios and the product
- **Watch out:** Denny (#20) and Palacios (#32, not top 20) tie to those Phoenix cases by surname and filing date only, and the charge is general fraud conspiracy, not health fraud.
- **Same story as:** menu F-033 (widened from the Bakersfield surgeon); deep3 #10 folds in

### 5. 19 police, fire, veteran and cancer-named political groups spent about $194M, mostly on fundraisers; $0.36M went to contributions
- **The number:** $193.7M by the groups' own IRS totals, 2014 to mid-2026 (itemized rows say $198.6M); 58% labelled fundraising or caging, 74% with telemarketing and mail; their donors are 15-74% of 13 look-alike FEC PACs' donor lists vs 6-9% for peer PACs
- **Tables:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_IRS527_SCHEDULE_B_EXPENDITURES`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__IRS527_8872_REPORTS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_IRS527_SCHEDULE_A_CONTRIBUTIONS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_INDIV_CONTRIBUTIONS`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_COMMITTEES`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__FED_FEC_PAC_SUMMARY`, `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_INDEPENDENT_EXPENDITURES`
- **Columns:** `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_IRS527_SCHEDULE_B_EXPENDITURES`: EIN, RECIPIENT_NAME, RECIPIENT_STATE, EXPENDITURE_DATE, EXPENDITURE_AMOUNT, EXPENDITURE_PURPOSE, FORM_ID_NUMBER · `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__IRS527_8872_REPORTS`: EIN, FORM_ID_NUMBER, PERIOD_BEGIN_DATE, PERIOD_END_DATE, TOTAL_SCHED_B · `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_IRS527_SCHEDULE_A_CONTRIBUTIONS`: EIN, CONTRIBUTOR_NAME, CONTRIBUTOR_ZIP, CONTRIBUTION_DATE, CONTRIBUTION_AMOUNT, FORM_ID_NUMBER · `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_INDIV_CONTRIBUTIONS`: CMTE_ID, DONOR_NAME, ZIP_CODE, MEMO_CD, TRANSACTION_AMT · `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_COMMITTEES`: CMTE_ID, CMTE_NM, CMTE_TP, TRES_NM · `LIBRARY_MARTS.POLITICS.POLITICS__FED_FEC_PAC_SUMMARY`: CMTE_ID, COVERAGE_END_DATE, TOTAL_RECEIPTS, TOTAL_DISBURSEMENTS, CONTRIBUTIONS_TO_OTHER_COMMITTEES, INDEPENDENT_EXPENDITURES · `LIBRARY_MARTS.FINANCE.FINANCE__FED_FEC_INDEPENDENT_EXPENDITURES`: SPE_ID, PAY, PUR, EXP_AMO, TRAN_ID, FILE_NUM, IS_SUPERSEDED
- **Joined on:** `EIN` across the IRS 527 files; donors on 5-digit ZIP + surname + first initial (Schedule A `CONTRIBUTOR_ZIP` / `CONTRIBUTOR_NAME` to FEC `ZIP_CODE` / `DONOR_NAME`); `CMTE_ID` to PAC totals; `SPE_ID` = `CMTE_ID` for outside spending
- **Grade:** B · narrowed · dossier j8 + j8_skeptic
- **Next:** Download FEC spending reports (Schedule B) for the 13 look-alike PACs and look for the same 25 calling and donation-processing vendors
- **Watch out:** The PACs' "7% to politics" is partly outside-spending paid to lead-list, caging and payment-processing vendors, so it overstates real support for candidates.
- **Same story as:** menu #5 (charity-named 527s)

### 6. Two mail-order suppliers took $593.6M of the $649.2M Medicare paid for one kind of wound dressing (alginate) in 2024
- **The number:** $593,632,181 of $649,233,082 (91.4%); one claim per patient a year, carrying 494 dressings (Sunshine Senior Solutions) and 359 (Almaz Med Supply) vs 55 per claim elsewhere; 5-11x peers per patient

- **Joined on:** `NPI` (supplier file `SUPLR_NPI` = ban list `NPI` = provider registry `NPI`)

### 7. 11% of San Antonio hospice ID numbers sit in "farms": one signer, 4+ hospice names, one address. Elsewhere: 0.5%
- **The number:** 31 of 282 active San Antonio hospice IDs (11.0%) vs 0.53% outside California, Houston and San Antonio; Houston 4-7%, California 3.3%; Houston's Suite 610 five trace to two signers, in a building holding 14 hospice IDs since 2008

### 8. Counties flooded with opioid pills in 2006-12 had 2x the overdose death rate in 2019-24, and were already dying faster
- **The number:** 40.8 vs 20.2 overdose deaths per 100k, 2019-24, across 1,832 counties of 20K+ people (2.0x; holds at 1.9-2.1x); Knox County TN #27 of 1,832 at 74.5; Food City #674 took 31% of Knox's 30mg+ oxycodone

- **Joined on:** 5-digit county FIPS (`BUYER_COUNTY_FIPS` = `GEOID` = `FIPS` = `COUNTY_FIPS`)
- **Grade:** B- · narrowed · dossier j4 + j4_skeptic (skeptic disagreed with the builder's B)
- **Next:** Check DEA actions and local news on Knoxville Food City #674, which alone took 31% of Knox County's 30mg+ oxycodone
- **Watch out:** Drop the AmerisourceBergen hook: it was the chain's normal wholesaler, and every distributor shipped these stores a strong mix. Split 30mg immediate-release from extended-release.
- **Same story as:** menu F-002 (pill counties); menu #21 (Food City #674)

## The full menu: 111 leads

Ledger findings plus coverage rounds 1 and 2, in the judge's rank order.

### 9. Two suppliers, Sunshine Senior Solutions and Almaz Med Supply, took 91% of Medicare's $649M for alginate wound dressings in 2024
- **The number:** $593.6M of the $649.2M Medicare paid for code A6197 in 2024 (91.4%), at 5-11x a normal supplier per patient depending on the yardstick.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_DURABLE_MEDICAL_EQUIPMENT_DEVICES_SUPPLIES_BY_SUPPL`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_DURABLE_MEDICAL_EQUIPMENT_DEVICES_SUPPLIES_BY_SUPPL`: SUPLR_NPI, SUPLR_PRVDR_LAST_NAME_ORG, SUPLR_PRVDR_CITY, SUPLR_PRVDR_STATE_ABRVTN, HCPCS_CD, HCPCS_DESC, AVG_SUPLR_MDCR_PYMT_AMT, AVG_SUPLR_MDCR_ALOWD_AMT, TOT_SUPLR_SRVCS, TOT_SUPLR_BENES
- **Joined on:** no join (one table; dollars = services × average payment)
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #1 · F-032 · scores size 5, novelty 4, defensible 4, days 7
- **Next:** Look for a DOJ or OIG action on Almaz Med Supply; Sunshine was banned in June 2026, Almaz is on no list.
- **Watch out:** Dollars are computed (services × average payment), and each patient got one claim a year of 359-494 dressings, not daily use.
- **Same story as:** dossier j2

### 10. Medicare approved $96.8M of skin-substitute wound grafts from one Bakersfield surgeon, Ravi Kapadia, for 28-35 patients in 2024
- **The number:** $96.8M allowed for 28-35 Medicare patients in 2024, about $2.6-3.3M a patient.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_PHYSICIAN_OTHER_PRACTITIONERS_BY_PROVIDER_AND_SERVI`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_MEDICARE_PHYSICIAN_OTHER_PRACTITIONERS_BY_PROVIDER_AND_SERVI`: RNDRNG_NPI, RNDRNG_PRVDR_LAST_ORG_NAME, RNDRNG_PRVDR_FIRST_NAME, RNDRNG_PRVDR_TYPE, RNDRNG_PRVDR_STATE_ABRVTN, HCPCS_CD, AVG_MDCR_ALOWD_AMT, TOT_SRVCS, TOT_BENES
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #2 · F-033 · scores size 5, novelty 3, defensible 4, days 7
- **Next:** Check court records (PACER) and news: DOJ went after skin-substitute billing in 2025.
- **Watch out:** The patient count is a range because patients are counted per billing code, and rows under 11 patients are hidden, so the totals are floors.
- **Same story as:** dossier j3

### 11. Phoenix's weather office logged 63% of the heat deaths in the federal storm file, then stopped filing heat after June 2024
- **The number:** 1,345 of 2,145 US direct heat deaths in 2018-23 came from the Phoenix office; national direct heat deaths went 557 (2023), 250 (2024), 99 (2025), and without Phoenix they still halved, 205 to 98.
- **Tables:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_NOAA_STORM_EVENTS`
- **Columns:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_NOAA_STORM_EVENTS`: YEAR, WFO, STATE, EVENT_TYPE, DEATHS_DIRECT, DEATHS_INDIRECT, BEGIN_YEARMONTH, CZ_NAME, EVENT_NARRATIVE, EPISODE_NARRATIVE
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #3 · round2 · scores size 3, novelty 4, defensible 4, days 4
- **Next:** Check the Weather Service's own yearly fatality summary and Maricopa County's 2024-25 heat-death reports to see if the gap is already public.
- **Watch out:** The drop is paperwork, not fewer deaths: Phoenix copied Maricopa County's counts from 2018 on and then stopped, and Tucson and Las Vegas moved deaths from direct to indirect in 2025.

### 12. SpaceX sites ran 3.2x their industry's injury rate in 2025, a third straight year; Ford about 1.9x big automakers
- **The number:** SpaceX's own sites 3.2x their industry (3.6x in 2024); Bath Iron Works 2.7x (3.0x); Ford 11.4 cases per 100 workers vs GM 6.5, Honda 6.8, Tesla 5.9, Toyota 5.2, about 1.9x.
- **Tables:** `LABOR__FED_OSHA_ITA_300A_SUMMARY_2025`, `LABOR__FED_OSHA_ITA_300A_SUMMARY_2024`
- **Columns:** `LABOR__FED_OSHA_ITA_300A_SUMMARY_2025`: COMPANY_NAME, ESTABLISHMENT_NAME, EIN, NAICS_CODE, TOTAL_DAFW_CASES, TOTAL_DJTR_CASES, TOTAL_OTHER_CASES, TOTAL_HOURS_WORKED, ANNUAL_AVERAGE_EMPLOYEES, TOTAL_DEATHS · `LABOR__FED_OSHA_ITA_300A_SUMMARY_2024`: COMPANY_NAME, ESTABLISHMENT_NAME, NAICS_CODE, TOTAL_DAFW_CASES, TOTAL_DJTR_CASES, TOTAL_OTHER_CASES, TOTAL_HOURS_WORKED, ANNUAL_AVERAGE_EMPLOYEES, TOTAL_DEATHS
- **Joined on:** no join (the two years are stacked; each company is compared with other sites in its own 6-digit NAICS_CODE)
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #4 · round1 · scores size 4, novelty 3, defensible 4, days 4
- **Next:** Check press on Ford and Bath Iron Works; SpaceX was already reported by Reuters in 2023.
- **Watch out:** Name tags catch contractors (SpaceX janitors and valet crews), "Ingalls" was really Newport News, and this updates F-035 to F-037 rather than finding something new.
- **Same story as:** menu #28 (F-036)

### 13. 19 police, fire, veteran and cancer political groups (527s) spent about $195M since 2014, three-quarters on telemarketers and fundraisers
- **The number:** $195M-$202M spent 2014 to mid-2026, depending on how amendment copies are dropped; about 74-77% to telemarketing and fundraising vendors and about $0.36M to anything called a contribution.

- **Joined on:** EIN
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #5 · round1 · scores size 4, novelty 3, defensible 4, days 10
- **Next:** Pull FEC filings for the two look-alike PACs (Police & Sheriffs Coalition PAC, Veterans In Defense of Liberty PAC) and check whether the press already covered them.
- **Watch out:** The strongest tie is 11 groups at about 90% fundraising and 9 paying BF-Telecom LLC since 2023; the shared Wisconsin address is only a Regus office.
- **Same story as:** dossier j8

### 14. One urgent-care chain, Fast Pace, holds 189 of 731 new for-profit rural health clinics opened after the 2021 pay law
- **The number:** 189 of the 731 new for-profit freestanding rural health clinics (26%), all enrolled from 2021-11-23 on; 192 counting 3 Louisiana clinics under the Fast Pace trade name.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_RURAL_HEALTH_CLINIC_ENROLLMENTS`, `HEALTH__FED_CMS_HOSPITAL_ENROLLMENTS`

- **Joined on:** ASSOCIATE_ID (is the clinic's owner also a hospital), with state agreeing
- **Grade:** confirmed · menu #6 · round2 · scores size 3, novelty 4, defensible 4, days 7
- **Next:** Check whether the Fast Pace angle is already covered, then find payment data to see if it captures the higher per-visit rate.

### 15. Share of ICE jail-hold requests (detainers) on people with a criminal conviction fell from 43% to 16%, FY23 to FY26
- **The number:** 43% (October-March FY23) to 16% (October-March FY26), same months both years; FY25 was 33% on matching months.
- **Tables:** `IMMIGRATION__FED_ICE_DETAINERS`
- **Columns:** `IMMIGRATION__FED_ICE_DETAINERS`: PERSON_HASH, DETAINER_PREPARE_DATE, DETAINER_PREPARED_CRIMINALITY, MOST_SERIOUS_CONVICTION_CHARGE, DUPLICATE_LIKELY
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** narrowed · menu #7 · F-027 · scores size 5, novelty 3, defensible 3, days 6
- **Next:** Find out whether the step down at the September-October 2025 file boundary is a policy change or a file artifact.
- **Watch out:** The drop lands exactly where one yearly file ends and the next begins, and the FY26 file was cut on 2026-03-11.

### 16. Counties that got the most opioid pills in 2006-12 had 2-2.7x the overdose death rate in 2019-24
- **The number:** 2.7x for the top pill-decile counties (ledger F-002); the j4 dossier rebuild, with suppressed years filled in, gets 2.02x (1.9-2.1x).
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_DEA_ARCOS`, `HEALTH__FED_CDC_INJURY_VIOLENCE_COUNTY`, `DIM_COUNTY`

- **Joined on:** county FIPS code (BUYER_COUNTY_FIPS = GEOID = COUNTY_FIPS)
- **Grade:** confirmed · menu #8 · F-002 · scores size 5, novelty 2, defensible 4, days 3
- **Next:** Split 30mg immediate-release pills from 40-80mg extended-release before charting (the j4 dossier's fix).

- **Same story as:** dossier j4

### 17. Federal car-safety regulators' Nissan Rogue phantom-braking probe has sat open 7 years, no recall; the typical probe takes 234 days
- **The number:** Probe PE19010, opened 2019-09-09, open 2,572 days with no recall; the median defect probe closes in 234 days.

- **Next:** Check NHTSA's web page for PE19010 against the flat file.
- **Watch out:** Complaint counts swing with the matching method (component code: 75 before, 273 after; complaint text: 135 before, 224 after), and by text the yearly rate fell after the probe opened.

### 19. New Mexico police cleared 22-23% of homicides in 2022-23, lowest in the nation, against 56% nationally
- **The number:** 22.3% (2022) and 23.2% (2023) by the FBI's state totals, lowest of 51 both years, vs 56% nationally; the fall was one year, 2018, from 59% to 34%.

### 20. People arrested 2+ years after arriving had ICE bond posted 7% of the time in Louisiana, 29% in Pennsylvania
- **The number:** 7% in Louisiana, 12% in Texas, 29% in Pennsylvania, 2023-25, for people arrested 2+ years after entry.

- **The number:** Texas averaged 3,530 a month in Jan-Mar 2026 (range 3,017-4,147) vs 2,797 in Jan-Mar 2024 (+26%); flat since the Dec 2024 jump.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__XC_GUTTMACHER_MONTHLY_ABORTION`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__XC_GUTTMACHER_MONTHLY_ABORTION`: STATE, MONTH, MEDIAN, LOWERBOUND, UPPERBOUND
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g07 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Read Guttmacher's method notes on its Jan 2025 switch to ranges before trusting the jump; then CDC WONDER births for a harm angle.
- **Watch out:** The jump sits right on a method seam: ban-state months before Jan 2025 carry no low-high range, every month after does. Guttmacher also publishes this trend itself.

### 211. Enbrel's two FDA-approved copies never went on sale; Medicare drug plans ran up $2.27B gross on brand Enbrel in 2022
- **The number:** $2,269.6M Part D gross drug cost for Enbrel in 2022 vs $0 for its copies (Erelzi 2016, Eticovo 2019, both listed discontinued).
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_FDA_PURPLE_BOOK`, `HEALTH__FED_CMS_PARTD_PRESCRIBERS`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__FED_FDA_PURPLE_BOOK`: license_type, ref_product_proper_name, proper_name, proprietary_name, applicant, bla_number, approval_date, marketing_status · `HEALTH__FED_CMS_PARTD_PRESCRIBERS`: generic_name, brand_name, data_year, total_claims, total_drug_cost
- **Joined on:** Purple Book proper_name / ref_product_proper_name (lower-cased FDA proper name, copies carry a four-letter suffix) = Part D generic_name; copy brand names searched in brand_name as a second check
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g09 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Check whether Enbrel's spend is already public through Medicare's drug-price negotiation list; then Purple Book to Part B 2024 on biosimilar billing codes for a current year.
- **Watch out:** Circular: Enbrel was picked because its copies read 'Disc', and a copy never sold has $0 sales by construction; pooled across all 11 molecules, copies took 0.89%, not the 16.6% median.

### 212. Kindred's 42 long-term hospitals book $178M a year more expense than Medicare's cost line; the peer gap is accounting
- **The number:** A fast-growing $178M-a-year gap between booked expense and Medicare's cost figure; on Medicare's cost line the Kindred-vs-peer margin gap shrinks from 9.4 points to 0.8.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_LTCH`, `HEALTH__FED_CMS_HCRIS`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__FED_CMS_LTCH`: CCN, PROVIDER_NAME, OWNERSHIP_TYPE, STATE · `HEALTH__FED_CMS_HCRIS`: PROVIDER_CCN, FISCAL_YEAR_END_DATE, FISCAL_YEAR_LENGTH_DAYS, NET_INCOME_FROM_SERVICE_TO_PATIENTS, NET_PATIENT_REVENUE, OCCUPANCY_RATE, NUMBER_OF_BEDS, STATE_CODE
- **Joined on:** LTCH CCN = HCRIS PROVIDER_CCN (304 of 311 land)
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g10 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Pull the cost-report adjustment worksheets for the 42; payments to related companies or the home office would bring it back as a B lead with a different story.
- **Watch out:** Kindred's -6.7% vs +2.7% margin is on booked expense; on Medicare's cost line the gap is 0.8 points and the same-state test falls from 11 of 13 states to 7 of 13.

### 213. In 2023, six California fiberglass shops put out about 40% of the state's reported cancer-list air pounds, mostly styrene
- **The number:** Styrene is about 55% of California's cancer-listed air pounds once sulfuric-acid mist (319,746 lbs) is counted, not 74.5%; the six shops about 40%, not half.
- **Tables:** `HEALTH__ST_OEHHA_PROPOSITION_65_LIST`, `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_TRI_BASIC_2023`
- **Columns:** `HEALTH__ST_OEHHA_PROPOSITION_65_LIST`: cas_no, chemical, type_of_toxicity, date_listed · `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_TRI_BASIC_2023`: c_40_cas, c_8_st, c_2_trifd, c_4_facility_name, c_37_chemical, c_46_carcinogen, c_50_unit_of_measure, c_51_5_1_fugitive_air, c_52_5_2_stack_air, c_30_primary_naics
- **Joined on:** Prop 65 cas_no = TRI c_40_cas (both trimmed), delisted and footnote rows dropped first
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g11 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Map the '---' group listings (acid mists, metal compounds) to TRI, then rerun on the 2015-2024 combined-emissions table, where TAG West reports 0 in 2024 and Xerxes halves.
- **Watch out:** The CAS join can't see group listings like sulfuric-acid mist (CAS '---'), which drops styrene to about 55%; and California is 7th of 44 states on styrene share, a pattern already found in deep-2 and g38.

### 214. This Congress passed 63 regular laws in its first 539 days, second-fewest since 2013; only the last Congress passed fewer
- **The number:** 63 laws that aren't rule repeals, renamings or spending bills by day 539 of the 119th (builder said 67); the 113th-117th had 93-145 at the same point, the 118th 43.

- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g12 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Join `FED_GOVINFO_BILL_COSPONSORS` to test whether bipartisan cosponsorship still predicts enactment in the 118th-119th.
- **Watch out:** "Regular law" is a title-keyword cut, not a classifier (it misses CRA votes titled "Disapproving the rule"); the fall 2025 shutdown (68 days with no laws) is an untested dull explanation.

### 215. 20 of 69 Georgia U.S. House races in 2010-18 had nobody running against the winner; nationally it was 4.8%
- **The number:** Georgia 20 of 69 (29%) with no runner-up; nation 4.8% (builder said 4.0%); median state 0.

### 216. Federal Election Commission totals for House Republicans' campaign arm skip its Sept 2024 report: $40.72M of spending
- **The number:** The NRCC's (C00075820) 2024 line is $21.88M off its own cash math because its Sept 2024 monthly ($40.72M spent, $18.85M raised) is missing from FEC totals; its own filings balance to the cent.

### 217. Only 16 of 83 retirement-eligible Republican-appointed judges stepped back in Trump's second term; 50 had already stayed through his first
- **The number:** 16 of 83 (19.3%) took senior status in the first 517 days; 50 of the 83 (60%) had already passed on it under Trump 1, vs 20% of Trump 1's own pool.

### 218. Sen. Richard Blumenthal filed all 69 of his stock-trade reports on paper, 2021-2026; the warehouse holds none of his trades
- **The number:** 69 of 69 periodic transaction reports on paper, Feb 2021 to Aug 2026; since 2024, 43 of the Senate's 44 paper filings are his.

- **The number:** $7.98M cash across 139 committees (median $366; 3 hold 59%; 32 terminated); Tallon for Congress paid out $1,391,737 in the 2024 cycle with $0 from individual donors.

### 220. RA Capital filed as director at 21 companies in 2024, OrbiMed 16, Baker Bros 13; eight firms drive biotech's lead

### 221. EPA sent Osage County, Oklahoma oil-field wells zero violation notices after April 2023, while still inspecting about 225 a year
- **The number:** Osage injection-well notices: 88 in 2022, 2 in 2023, 0 since April 2023; formal cases on the same wells held at 14-18 a year.

### 222. A third of small, non-exempt US-listed foreign companies missed a new March 2026 insider-stock deadline by 13+ days

- **The number:** Builder: at most $22.5M (12.5%) to politics, 2017-24; 73% of the itemized ad money paid a phone and caging vendor block, so real political spending is closer to 4%. That six-vendor block is paid only by sympathy-named committees, 24 of 24.

- **The number:** 24 of 66 facilities (36%, 17-36% depending on the cutoff) with a high-priority violation from before July 2024, unresolved, no formal action ever; on Title V major sources alone, 5 of 40.
- **Tables:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_PROGRAMS`, `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_VIOLATION_HISTORY`, `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FORMAL_ACTIONS`, `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FACILITIES`
- **Columns:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_PROGRAMS`: PGM_SYS_ID, PROGRAM_CODE, AIR_OPERATING_STATUS_CODE · `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_VIOLATION_HISTORY`: PGM_SYS_ID, ENF_RESPONSE_POLICY_CODE, HPV_DAYZERO_DATE, HPV_RESOLVED_DATE · `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FORMAL_ACTIONS`: PGM_SYS_ID, SETTLEMENT_ENTERED_DATE, PENALTY_AMOUNT · `ENVIRONMENT__FED_EPA_ICIS_AIR_ICIS_AIR_FACILITIES`: PGM_SYS_ID, STATE
- **Joined on:** PGM_SYS_ID (air-program facility ID)
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g37 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Records request to Nebraska's environment department (NDEE) for orders on the 24; no orders would lift it to B.
- **Watch out:** ADM Columbus isn't in the 24 and only 4 of the 7 named ethanol plants are; 19 of the 24 aren't Title V sources, and a records gap is likely (Nebraska's formal actions in the database fell 89% at the 2014 system switch).

### 230. Diamondback's Permian Basin frack jobs drew 1.6-2.3 times the fresh-groundwater share of other reporting drillers in 2025
- **The number:** Gap 1.6-2.3x in 2025 against operators that report water, 1.2x across all years; the builder's 40.5% vs 10.4% (about 4x) didn't hold, and "22% of wells" is really 9.3%.
- **Tables:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_FRACFOCUS_WATER_SOURCE`, `ENVIRONMENT__FED_FRACFOCUS_DISCLOSURE_LIST`, `LIBRARY_RAW.LANDING.FED_FRACFOCUS_DISCLOSURE_LIST`
- **Columns:** `ENVIRONMENT__FED_FRACFOCUS_WATER_SOURCE`: DISCLOSURE_ID, DESCRIPTION, PERCENT, OPERATOR_NAME · `ENVIRONMENT__FED_FRACFOCUS_DISCLOSURE_LIST`: DISCLOSURE_ID, API_NUMBER, OPERATOR_NAME, STATE_NAME, COUNTY_NAME, TOTAL_BASE_WATER_VOLUME, FF_VERSION, LATITUDE, LONGITUDE · `LIBRARY_RAW.LANDING.FED_FRACFOCUS_DISCLOSURE_LIST`: DISCLOSUREID, JOBSTARTDATE, JOBENDDATE
- **Joined on:** DISCLOSURE_ID (every water row lands); raw DISCLOSUREID for job dates, which the mart dropped
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g38 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Set Diamondback's own fresh-water figure from its 2024-2025 sustainability report against these filings.
- **Watch out:** The 4x was basin mix (Diamondback's Midland Basin wells vs a peer group 71% in the Delaware Basin) plus a groundwater-only count; the biggest home-county peers file no water rows at all.

### 231. The Pentagon's tech agency renewed Ivanti's often-hacked phone-management software each spring: $21.8M+ since 2023, on a fixed calendar
- **The number:** $21,771,170 on 6 DISA awards, May 2023 to Apr 2026 (skeptic: really 9 DISA awards; federal EPMM spending at least ~$43.8M); 7 exploited EPMM holes listed in that window.
- **Tables:** `JUSTICE__FED_CISA_KEV`, `ECONOMICS__FED_USASPENDING_CONTRACTS_FULL_R2`
- **Columns:** `JUSTICE__FED_CISA_KEV`: CVE_ID, VENDOR_PROJECT, PRODUCT, DATE_ADDED, DUE_DATE, KNOWN_RANSOMWARE_CAMPAIGN_USE · `ECONOMICS__FED_USASPENDING_CONTRACTS_FULL_R2`: CONTRACT_AWARD_UNIQUE_KEY, AWARD_ID_PIID, ACTION_DATE, FEDERAL_ACTION_OBLIGATION, AWARDING_SUB_AGENCY_NAME, RECIPIENT_NAME, TRANSACTION_DESCRIPTION, PERIOD_OF_PERFORMANCE_START_DATE
- **Joined on:** no shared key: vendor and product names (KEV VENDOR_PROJECT, PRODUCT) text-matched to award TRANSACTION_DESCRIPTION and RECIPIENT_NAME, then every action pulled by CONTRACT_AWARD_UNIQUE_KEY
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g51 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** DISA's justification (J&A) documents for HC108425F0287, plus FPDS product codes.
- **Watch out:** The "$8,043,000 four days after the listing" is the yearly license renewal for a June 1 start (DISA has ordered in Mar-May every year since 2017); "paid" should read "obligated".

### 232. US coal power rose 85 terawatt-hours (13%) in 2025, about 1.8 times every other country's coal rise combined
- **The number:** +84.99 TWh (+13.03%); the 23 other risers among 89 countries with 2025 data added 36.26 TWh (2.3x), about 1.8x once Ember's Asia residual for missing countries is added.
- **Tables:** `ENERGY__INTL_EMBER_ELEC`
- **Columns:** `ENERGY__INTL_EMBER_ELEC`: COUNTRY, AREA_TYPE, YEAR, CATEGORY, SUBCATEGORY, VARIABLE, UNIT, VALUE, YOY_ABSOLUTE_CHANGE, YOY_PCT_CHANGE
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g60 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Check the early 2025 figures against EIA's final annual numbers and the federal orders that kept retiring coal plants running.
- **Watch out:** It is a rebound: 2023-24 lost 179 TWh when gas was cheap, and 2025 wins back 85 while staying 94 TWh below 2022. 105 countries, Indonesia included, have no 2025 data.

### 233. Texas gas plants able to switch to oil rose from 4.9% to 6.0% of capacity after the 2021 freeze
- **The number:** ERCOT 3,508 MW of 63,770 MW gas winter capacity (5.5%; 6.0% on the generator flag), up from 4.9% before Uri; SPP fell 17.2% to 13.6%, MISO 18.5% to 17.8%.
- **Tables:** `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_3_5_MULTIFUEL`, `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_3_1_GENERATOR`, `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_2_PLANT`
- **Columns:** `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_3_5_MULTIFUEL`: PLANT_CODE, GENERATOR_ID, ENERGY_SOURCE_1, SWITCH_BETWEEN_OIL_AND_NATURAL_GAS, NET_WINTER_CAPACITY_WITH_OIL_MW, WINTER_CAPACITY_MW, UTILITY_NAME, OPERATING_YEAR, AIR_PERMIT_LIMITS, STORAGE_LIMITS · `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_3_1_GENERATOR`: PLANT_CODE, STATUS, ENERGY_SOURCE_1, WINTER_CAPACITY_MW, SWITCH_BETWEEN_OIL_AND_NATURAL_GAS, OPERATING_YEAR · `ENERGY__FED_EIA860_2_PLANT`: PLANT_CODE, BALANCING_AUTHORITY_CODE
- **Joined on:** PLANT_CODE (plant to its grid through BALANCING_AUTHORITY_CODE)
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g63 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Primary check on the 2020 drop and Luminant's post-Uri oil add, using `LIBRARY_RAW.LANDING.FED_EIA860_GENERATOR_Y2019` to `_Y2023`; 2,220 MW flipped to "N" with no retirement.
- **Watch out:** "Less than half of SPP and MISO" is north vs south: SPP's southern states sit at 4.7% (Oklahoma 1.0%) and MISO's at 6.7%; the switch flags are self-reported by operators.

### 234. Four unnamed traders held 69.7% of sell positions in one mid-Atlantic power futures contract in early 2026, a record
- **The number:** Top-4 share of the short side, PJM Western Hub off-peak day-ahead futures on ICE: 69.7% in 2026 Q1 (peak week 71.8% on 2026-03-10) vs 47.4% in 2024 Q1, but only about 5 points over the 2014-15 highs.
- **Tables:** `EDUCATION__FED_CFTC_COT_FUTURES`
- **Columns:** `EDUCATION__FED_CFTC_COT_FUTURES`: CFTC_CONTRACT_MARKET_CODE, MARKET_AND_EXCHANGE_NAMES, AS_OF_DATE_IN_FORM_YYYY_MM_DD, CONCENTRATION_GROSS_LT_4_TDR_SHORT_ALL, OPEN_INTEREST_ALL, TRADERS_TOTAL_ALL, TRADERS_COMMERCIAL_SHORT_ALL
- **Joined on:** no join
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g66 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** FERC's Constellation-Calpine merger docket and PJM market-monitor reports, to line their dates up with the 2025 Q1 step.
- **Watch out:** One exchange only: the same product on Nodal Exchange sits near its low (~32%), 2024 is the series' lowest point, and the CFTC never names the traders.

### 235. FEMA couldn't confirm occupancy on 28.6% of downtown Lahaina's fire-aid registrations; adjusted, the gap to neighbors is 7.5-9 points
- **The number:** Tract 314.04: 3,261 registrations for 3,223 residents (1.01, 38 over the line); 71.4% verified vs 86.3% and 88.1% in the other two burn tracts.
- **Tables:** `DIM_TRACT`, `HOUSING__FED_FEMA_IA_HOUSING_REGISTRATIONS`
- **Columns:** `DIM_TRACT`: TRACT_GEOID, POPULATION_2020 · `HOUSING__FED_FEMA_IA_HOUSING_REGISTRATIONS`: DISASTER_NUMBER, CENSUS_GEOID, REGISTRATION_ID, VERIFIED_OCCUPANCY, PRIMARY_RESIDENCE, OWN_RENT, RESIDENCE_TYPE, DECLARATION_DATE, APPLIED_DATE
- **Joined on:** first 11 digits of FEMA CENSUS_GEOID = DIM_TRACT TRACT_GEOID (disasters declared 2022+, 99.6% land)
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g72 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Ask FEMA or its inspector general for the reason codes behind the ~930 unverified 314.04 registrations.
- **Watch out:** About 40% of the unverified said it wasn't their primary home; the rest rests on one field (VERIFIED_OCCUPANCY) whose reason codes can't be read, and unburned neighbor 315.03 verifies lower still.

### 236. Four people barred from Medicare show up in the offshore leaks at matching US addresses, out of 1,611 name hits
- **The number:** 1,611 name-only matches to the federal health-care exclusion list, 4 left with street or ZIP agreeing; 3 tied to Cook Islands asset-protection trusts.
- **Tables:** `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_ADDRESSES`, `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_RELATIONSHIPS`, `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_OFFICERS`, `HEALTH__FED_HHS_OIG_LEIE`, `PROCUREMENT__FED_SAM_EXCLUSIONS`
- **Columns:** `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_ADDRESSES`: NODE_ID, ADDRESS, NAME, COUNTRY_CODES · `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_RELATIONSHIPS`: NODE_ID_START, NODE_ID_END, REL_TYPE · `CORPORATE_REGISTRY__FED_ICIJ_OFFSHORELEAKS_OFFICERS`: NODE_ID, NAME, COUNTRY_CODES, SOURCE_LEAK · `HEALTH__FED_HHS_OIG_LEIE`: FIRST_NAME, LAST_NAME, MIDDLE_NAME, ADDRESS, CITY, ZIP, EXCLUSION_TYPE, EXCLUSION_DATE · `PROCUREMENT__FED_SAM_EXCLUSIONS`: FIRST_NAME, LAST_NAME, CITY, STATE, ZIP

- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g75 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** One court check on Scott Gregory Roix (restitution vs his 3 BVI companies), the one thread that could promote.
- **Watch out:** Golden held no trust (his nodes are middleman, protector and "nominated person"); the leak-vs-others comparison measures ZIP completeness, not behavior; Bressi and Roix match only at ZIP level through SAM.

### 237. Justice Department's Epstein file list for Data Set 10 shrank from 10,071 to 5,569 pages, March to late May 2026
- **The number:** Data Set 10: 10,071 to 5,569 pages (about 225,000 file links out of the listing) by Mar 8; Data Set 9: 10,675 to 770 for about 4 weeks; both back May 22-23.

### 238. Coal operators who hand off a mine leave 16.6% of safety fines unpaid, vs 13.0% for those who close it
- **The number:** Builder: 28.7% unpaid at mines with 3+ operators vs 3.7% at one-operator mines; fair comparison, without Blackjewel/Revelation and Brody: 16.6% vs 13.0%, about 3.6 points.
- **Tables:** `LABOR__FED_MSHA_MINES`, `LABOR__FED_MSHA_VIOLATIONS`
- **Columns:** `LABOR__FED_MSHA_MINES`: MINE_ID, CURRENT_OPERATOR_ID, CURRENT_CONTROLLER_ID, CURRENT_CONTROLLER_NAME, CURRENT_MINE_STATUS, COAL_METAL_IND, STATE · `LABOR__FED_MSHA_VIOLATIONS`: MINE_ID, VIOLATOR_ID, VIOLATOR_NAME, VIOLATION_OCCUR_DATE, AMOUNT_DUE, AMOUNT_PAID
- **Joined on:** MINE_ID; violator IDs kept only in the operator-ID format, which drops contractors
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g82 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Join a bankruptcy table (not landed) to split bankrupt operators from walk-aways; report every figure with and without the two big IDs.
- **Watch out:** A one-operator mine can't carry departed-operator debt, so the builder's peer couldn't fail; Blackjewel/Revelation (0100767) and Brody (0070543) carry the dollars, and it's Central Appalachia only.

### 239. 13 tankers seen in U.S. waters one January 2024 week now sit on government sanctions lists, most renamed
- **The number:** Older tankers (IMO below 9400000): 10 of 218 on government lists (4.6%, not 6.0%); newer: 3 of 732; 3 of the builder's 16 aren't sanctioned.
- **Tables:** `MARITIME__FED_NOAA_AIS`, `JUSTICE__FED_OFAC_SDN`, `JUSTICE__INTL_OPENSANCTIONS_DEFAULT`
- **Columns:** `MARITIME__FED_NOAA_AIS`: MMSI, IMO_NORMALIZED, VESSEL_NAME, VESSEL_TYPE_CODE, LENGTH_METERS, BASE_DATETIME, SPEED_OVER_GROUND, LATITUDE, LONGITUDE · `JUSTICE__FED_OFAC_SDN`: IMO_NUMBER, SDN_NAME, PROGRAM, IS_VESSEL · `JUSTICE__INTL_OPENSANCTIONS_DEFAULT`: NAME, ENTITY_TYPE, IDENTIFIERS, DATASETS, FIRST_SEEN
- **Joined on:** 7-digit IMO hull number (check digit must pass) = OFAC IMO_NUMBER or an OpenSanctions IDENTIFIERS entry
- **Grade:** C · narrowed · deep pass 3 g82 · below the menu cut
- **Next:** Get a world-fleet count of older tankers for a base rate, and check each hull's official designation notice (dates here are OpenSanctions "first seen").
- **Watch out:** No world base rate (the U.S.-feed rate may sit below it), and "served U.S. refineries" is backwards: hits are highest among ships that never stopped (5 of 50) vs 3 of 134 that tied up at a U.S. berth.
